\paragraph{Coarse-anchoring of the persistence interval.} The bare two-point
rule is made robust to warm-start path dependence by requiring the accepted
interval to be \emph{coarse-anchored}: the region's \emph{coarsest} multi-cluster
partition must itself begin a persistent run of length $\ge P_{\mathrm{persist}}$.
The motivation is the scale-space picture above: an intrinsic feature is born (in
the coarse-to-fine direction) at its coarse-end death $v_{\mathrm{death}}^+$ and,
by Gaussian non-enhancement of the smoothed density, persists monotonically
toward finer scales, so one expects it to be present already at the scale where
the region first ceases to be a single cluster. A candidate partition that
appears only at finer scales---after the coarser scales have churned through
mutually non-agreeing multi-cluster states---is treated as a resolution artifact
of the warm-started refit and rejected. Formally, letting $j_0$ be the coarsest
grid index with $K_{j_0}\ge 2$, the interval is accepted iff its run length
$L_{j_0}\ge P_{\mathrm{persist}}$, and then $v_{\mathrm{death}}^+=\tau_{j_0}$;
otherwise the region carries no coarse-anchored feature and is terminal. This is
hardening direction~(ii) below, and it is the canonical rule
(\texttt{coarse\_anchored} in the persistence configuration, S14.3).

We stress that this is an \emph{operational} rule, not a grid-invariant
consequence of scale-space theory. Non-enhancement is a statement about the
extrema of the smoothed density family, and does not transfer verbatim to the
first \emph{detected} partition of a warm-started scaffold-plus-AP pipeline on a
discrete $\log\tau$ grid. Two known trade-offs follow. (a)~\emph{False negatives
from a transient coarse blip:} if the coarsest multi-cluster grid point is a
one-off partition that disagrees with a genuine persistent block just finer than
it, the region is declared terminal even though real sub-structure exists just
below; the recursion does not revisit it. (b)~\emph{Grid sensitivity:} inserting
or removing a coarse grid point can move $j_0$ and flip an accept to a reject.
Both are acceptable at Stage~1---the rule is deliberately conservative, and the
Stage~2 evidence gate is the ultimate arbiter of acceptance---but they are the
reason $P_{\mathrm{persist}}$, $\theta_{\mathrm{ovl}}$, and
\texttt{coarse\_anchored} are all labeled operational in S14.3.

\paragraph{The warm-start false positive and the residual limitation.}
The controller sweep is \emph{warm-started}: each grid point inherits the
previous scaffold, so partition labelings are path-dependent. On a uniform
manifold this can lock a fragment partition in place over a short interval even
though no intrinsic feature exists. Empirically (circle, $8$ grid points, with
the S2.6.1 stand-ins disabled), the matched-Jaccard overlaps between adjacent
arc-partitions are mostly low ($0.17$--$0.33$, as expected), but an isolated
fine-end pair can coincide at $\approx0.61$ --- enough to satisfy the bare
$P_{\mathrm{persist}}=2$ rule at $\theta_{\mathrm{ovl}}=0.5$ and admit a spurious
split, whereas an independent \emph{cold-start} refit of the same scales removes
the block entirely. A genuine multi-modal region instead persists from the
\emph{coarsest} grid point with high, stable overlaps ($\gtrsim0.68$) over a run
of length $\ge3$. Coarse-anchoring (above) removes exactly this failure: because
the circle's coarsest multi-cluster partition does not itself persist, the ring
is correctly judged a single feature. Under a full removal of the S2.6.1
stand-ins with the persistence accept-gate on, coarse-anchoring reduces the
circle from tens of spurious leaves to a single leaf and leaves the
$3$-coarse/$6$-fine hierarchical Gaussian at its correct six terminal leaves.

It is \emph{not yet} sufficient to retire the stand-ins entirely. A developable
manifold whose coarsest scaffold partition is a small number of arcs (the swiss
roll: three arcs at the coarsest grid point) can produce a coarse-anchored run
whose adjacent overlap sits just above $\theta_{\mathrm{ovl}}$ ($\approx0.57$),
so coarse-anchoring admits it and the region over-fragments without the
boundary-refinement and tiny-cluster-absorption stand-ins. The residual is thus
narrowed from a fine-end warm-start artifact (now resolved) to \emph{marginal
coarse-scale arc-persistence on developable manifolds}. Hardening directions
considered for this residual: (i) require the complementary Stage~2
Dirichlet--multinomial evidence gate (\hyperref[sec:si-dm-evidence]{S3.4}) as the
acceptance null --- an arc-partition of a connected manifold fails a Bayes-factor
margin test where a marginal overlap does not; (ii$'$) require the candidate
coarse-anchored interval to survive an independent \emph{cold-start}
recomputation of the same grid interval, so acceptance rests on
path-\emph{independent} persistence; (iii) require a longer minimal run or a
higher \emph{mean} interval overlap. Threshold-only tightening of
$\theta_{\mathrm{ovl}}$ (iii) is brittle and is not recommended.

Direction (ii$'$) was implemented (\texttt{PersistenceConfig.cold\_start\_recheck},
default off) and \emph{empirically refuted as an acceptance gate}. The
refutation is instructive: cold single-$\tau$ fits have high
\emph{resolution-level} variance, because an independently seeded scaffold at a
coarse cap may settle at a different level of the recursion hierarchy than the
warm sweep reaches there. On the $3$-coarse/$6$-fine hierarchical Gaussian the
warm coarse anchor is a stable $3$-way partition, but independent cold-start
refits of the two anchoring grid points return a $6$-way and a $3$-way partition
(matched overlap $\approx0.27<\theta_{\mathrm{ovl}}$), so the interval ``fails''
the recheck and the genuine root split is rejected; under a full removal of the
S2.6.1 stand-ins with the recheck on, the hierarchy collapses to a single leaf.
The matched-Jaccard overlap of independently fit partitions therefore cannot
separate a true absence of structure from ordinary cross-scale resolution
variance --- which is precisely the discrimination the Bayes-factor \emph{margin}
of the S3.4 evidence gate provides. This refutes the specific overlap-based
recheck, not every conceivable path-independence diagnostic; but direction (i)
is the only currently validated resolution, so until it lands
$P_{\mathrm{persist}}$ is an operational default and the S2.6.1 stand-ins remain
the operating acceptance path.

\paragraph{Finer-than-$\tau^\star$ research (proposed / operational;
OPEN\_ISSUES~\#44).}
When a region is $K{=}1$ (or non-persistent) at its characteristic $\tau^\star$,
the acceptance gate --- not a $K{\ge}2$ heuristic --- owns further descent. An
optional finer-than-$\tau^\star$ re-search
(\texttt{RecursionConfig.allow\_finer\_research}, default off) may probe
$\tau\in(\tau_{\min},\,\tau^\star\cdot\texttt{finer\_tau\_cap\_ratio}]$ for a
multi-cluster proposal; accepted children still require the active persistence
and/or DM gates. Enabling this path without
\texttt{require\_persistent\_split}, a modest
\texttt{max\_finer\_scale\_steps} budget (preferably $\le4$), and
$\texttt{min\_samples}\ge80$ is unsupported for uniform manifolds: the flag
alone shatters the circle ($\sim\!21$ leaves), while that persist pairing keeps
circle and swiss-roll at one leaf. Disconnected multi-component scenes can still
require many grid steps below the coarse $\tau^\star$ ($\sim\!80\times$ finer on
nested shells / linked tori); the finer path is therefore a proposal-path
prototype, not a licence to change the default acceptance path.

Lifted connected-component prepass
(\texttt{prefer\_disconnected\_prepass}, default off) misses concentric shells
that remain radius-bridged on the Hebbian graph. Proposal-path radial prepasses
(all default off; S14.3) address that geometry:
\texttt{prefer\_radial\_gap\_prepass} (centroid radial gap),
\texttt{prefer\_radial\_band\_prepass} (histogram-trough / contiguous peak-support
mask before the gap),
\texttt{prefer\_noncentroid\_radial\_band\_prepass} (coordinate-median origin
plus relative trough-depth gate
\texttt{finer\_radial\_min\_trough\_rel}), and
\texttt{prefer\_signal\_density\_band\_prepass} (keep the top
\texttt{finer\_signal\_density\_keep\_frac} of nodes by knn-density residual
against a smooth radial-histogram expectation, then run the band gap on the
kept subset). A non-radial dual,
\texttt{prefer\_pca\_axis\_gap\_prepass} (default off; OPEN\_ISSUES~\#44),
projects scaffold positions onto the leading principal component and takes the
largest balanced 1-D gap (same size / min-gap-ratio / $Q{>}0$ gates as the
radial gap), plus a centroid-separation gate
$\|c_0-c_1\|/(\mathrm{rms}_0+\mathrm{rms}_1)\ge1$ that rejects PC1 diameter
cuts on concentric scaffolds. Unit offset rings recover under that cue;
interlocking linked-tori remain unrecovered under end-to-end
persist$+$pca --- do not flip awaiting. Under the unit harness
($n_{\mathrm{per}}=64$, $n_{\mathrm{seeds}}=8$, persist on,
$\texttt{max\_finer\_scale\_steps}\le12$), signal-density recovers nested shells
at $2$ leaves / $\mathrm{ARI}{=}1.0$ while the circle stays one leaf at
steps$\le8$; band and noncentroid alone remain unrecovered on that harness.
These flags stay proposal-path / default-off pending linked-tori and swiss-roll
guards and awaiting-flip confirmation --- they are \emph{not} acceptance-path,
and do not yet retire the S2.6.1 stand-ins.

\paragraph{Hollow-edge / empty-region evidence (proposed; OPEN\_ISSUES~\#44).}
\label{sec:si-hollow-edge}
Geometry-specific prepasses (radial / PCA / tube / spectral) impose a global
coordinate and still miss interlocking linked-tori. The deeper diagnosis is a
\emph{category error} in the word ``scale'': \emph{resolution} scale (the
$\tau$ at which a feature's support is resolved; $L{=}1$ up-crossing, S2.5.1)
is distinct from \emph{separation} scale (the $\tau$ at which two supports
become distinguishable to the scaffold graph). Disconnection of supports is
scale-free and topological --- the nested-spheres scaffold at $\tau{=}0.27$ and
$\tau{=}0.004$ shares the same $64$ nodes --- so finer-than-$\tau^\star$
descent cannot create information the statistic cannot see. Moreover, at
equilibrium every scaffold-side statistic self-normalizes away the geometry it
was meant to measure; structural tests must therefore be \emph{data-side}.

For a lifted edge $(i,j)$ with endpoints $\mathbf x_i,\mathbf x_j$ and length
$L$, the batch hollow-edge probe asks whether data occupy the region the edge
spans:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item $n_{\mathrm{mid}}$ = number of samples within radius
  $r{=}f_{\mathrm{mid}}L$ of the midpoint $(\mathbf x_i+\mathbf x_j)/2$
  (lens / Gabriel-ball approximation);
  \item $n_{\mathrm{end}}$ = mean of the same counts around $\mathbf x_i$ and
  $\mathbf x_j$;
  \item hollowness ratio $H(i,j)=n_{\mathrm{mid}}/(n_{\mathrm{end}}+\varepsilon)$.
\end{itemize}
Within-support edges have $H=\mathrm{O}(1)$; void bridges have
$H\approx\mathrm{tissue}/\mathrm{signal}\approx0$. Cut edges with $H<h_0$
\emph{before} connected-components / AP clustering, at a hollowness-sensitive
scaffold scale, with \emph{no} finer descent. The ratio form is robust to a
uniform tissue background. The proper null is Poisson: under a locally uniform
manifold of density $\rho$ estimated from the endpoint counts, the mid-ball
count is $\mathrm{Poisson}(\rho\,V_{\mathrm{ball}})$, so ``hollow'' is a
calibrated likelihood-ratio (or e-value) decision --- $h_0$ is
\emph{acceptance-path} once calibrated (S14.3). The mid-ball fraction
$f_{\mathrm{mid}}$, a minimum endpoint mass floor $n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min}$, and
any online EWMA histogram bins remain \emph{proposal-path} operational
defaults. Naive $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}1/4$ (theory-note $L/4$) false-hollows when
$n_{\mathrm{end}}\approx 0$ ($H\to 0$ mass-false-positive); the landed probe
(A2-T27) uses $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}0.35$, provisional $h_0{=}0.35$, and
$n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min}{=}0.5$ jointly with Gabriel fallback --- seed-$0$
nested$+$tori majors hit, multi-seed fragile. At
$n_{\mathrm{end}}<n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min}$, fall back to the classical Gabriel
empty-diameter test (cut when the open diameter ball contains no data), which
needs no density estimate (Gabriel \& Sokal~1969; Toussaint~1980
relative-neighborhood / lens variant).

An online Stage-1-native form records the projection parameter $t\in[0,1]$ of
each co-activating sample onto the segment and maintains a tiny EWMA histogram
(ends / middle). Connected support $\Rightarrow$ mid-bin mass $\mathrm{O}(1/3)$;
a bridge $\Rightarrow$ bimodal end-concentration. That mid-bin mass is the
natural $M_{\mathrm{transit}}$ factor in the S2.6.1 edge weight
$W_v=K_v A_{\mathrm{sym}} M_{\mathrm{transit}}$.

Predicted mechanism (frozen-scaffold probe first): nested shells cut
shell-to-shell bridges $\to\mathrm{cc}{=}2$; linked tori cut every A--B void
bridge without a global coordinate $\to\mathrm{cc}{=}2$; manifold-zoo junctions
carry data $\to$ no cut; swiss-roll inter-wrap shortcuts cut while the sheet
stays connected. Density structure (blob hierarchies) remains persistence
(S2.6.2) $+$ DM (S2.6.3); resolution remains $L{=}1$.

\paragraph{Cluster-tree framing.}
What Stage~1's structural layer estimates is Hartigan's density cluster tree.
Classical anchors: Chaudhuri--Dasgupta (2010) robust single linkage (consistent
cluster-tree estimator; connectivity radius $+$ density floor); ToMATo
(Chazal--Guibas--Oudot--Skraba~2013) (density-mode merging gated by persistence
--- formalizes the S2.6.2 gate); Stuetzle (2003) runt pruning (principled
cleanup aligned with eventual \#27 stand-in deletion). Framing the $\tau$ sweep
as cluster-tree estimation turns disconnected supports into ordinary high-level
tree splits and supplies known consistency results.

\paragraph{Landed proposal-path wiring and protocol.}
Module \texttt{proteus.stage1.edge\_evidence} exposes
\texttt{hollowness\_scores} / \texttt{prune\_hollow\_edges} behind
\texttt{HollowEdgeConfig}; recursion consumes them via
\texttt{RecursionConfig.prefer\_hollow\_edge\_prepass} (default off; S14.3)
together with operational knobs
\texttt{hollow\_mid\_radius\_frac}, \texttt{hollow\_h0},
\texttt{hollow\_min\_end\_count}, \texttt{hollow\_gabriel\_fallback},
\texttt{hollow\_require\_gabriel\_and\_h} (conjunction; default off; A2-T31),
\texttt{hollow\_use\_a4\_primary} (A4 primary preset; default off; A2-T33),
\texttt{hollow\_mst\_critical\_only} (MST$\cap$hollow; default off; A2-T34),
\texttt{hollow\_bridge\_critical\_only} (graph-bridge$\cap$hollow; default
off; A2-T34-followon), and soft-capacity knobs
\texttt{HollowEdgeConfig.soft\_capacity\_only} /
\texttt{soft\_capacity\_frac} /
\texttt{soft\_capacity\_method} (Brandes betweenness or bridge-mass
$\ge f\cdot\max\mathrm{score}$; default off / $f{=}0.25$ /
\texttt{"betweenness"}; A2-T37/T39). When the prepass
fires, Q is scored on the \emph{pruned} edge set. Protocol status (do
\emph{not} flip awaiting):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Frozen-scaffold probe (A2-T27): theory direction supported; $L/4$ alone
  fails; joint $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}0.35$, $h_0{=}0.35$, Gabriel fallback is
  seed-fragile.
  \item Unit wiring $+$ swiss / circle / zoo guards under the hit config
  (A2-T28): inter-wrap shortcuts cut, sheet $\mathrm{cc}$ stays $1$.
  \item Adversarial-null ROC harness (A4-T18): connected sheets with density
  gradients / curvature must not cut; gap tissue must cut; calibration of
  acceptance-path $h_0$ still open (Poisson-null / Youden on ROC grid).
  \item Fuller suite persist$(+\mathrm{dm})$ $+$ hollow, \emph{no} finer
  descent (A2-T29): uniforms / zoo safe; nested unrecovered at scale-search
  $\tau^\star$; linked-tori may spurious-split with near-zero ARI. Fixed-$\tau$
  oracle $K{=}2$ is \emph{not} recovery (sample ARI$\sim$chance; A2-T30).
  Mechanism attribution: at $\tau^\star$ hollow rarely fires --- scale-search
  $\tau^\star$ $\neq$ hollowness-sensitive $\tau$. Multi-$\tau$ scan alone
  $\neq$ recovery; Gabriel OR-$H$ drives spurious $K{=}2$ at probe $\tau$.
  \item Conjunction / no-Gabriel suppressors (A2-T31..T32):
  \texttt{require\_gabriel\_and\_h} (cut iff $H{<}h_0$ \emph{and}
  Gabriel-empty) and \texttt{hollow\_require\_persistent\_agree} both default
  off; conjunction suppresses probe $K{=}2$ seed-stably. Prefer these or
  calibrated no-Gabriel over raising $n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min}$ alone (which
  increases Gabriel usage).
  \item A4-primary apply + MST-critical + sample-ARI harness (A2-T33..T35;
  flags default off): \texttt{a4\_roc\_primary\_config} /
  \texttt{hollow\_use\_a4\_primary} applies the A4 primary
  $(f_{\mathrm{mid}},h_0,n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min},\mathrm{Gabriel})
  {=}(0.5,0.7,0.5,\mathrm{False})$; uniforms / zoo stay safe, nested / tori
  unrecovered on sample ARI. \texttt{mst\_critical\_only} /
  \texttt{hollow\_mst\_critical\_only} intersects hollow cuts with the
  Euclidean MST edge set (conservative bridge proxy): nested@$0.27$ majors
  $\le 1$ vs default $K{=}2$ with ARI$\sim$chance. Recovery harness locks
  empty-ball mid$0.35$ and MST non-cut-set failure modes; $K{=}2$ alone is
  \emph{not} recovery --- assert sample ARI.
  \item Bridge-critical cut-set (A2-T34-followon; flags default off):
  \texttt{HollowEdgeConfig.bridge\_critical\_only} /
  \texttt{hollow\_bridge\_critical\_only} intersects hollow cuts with
  \emph{graph-theoretic bridges} of the lifted undirected graph (edges whose
  removal increases the CC count). Bridges $\subseteq$ every spanning tree, so
  this is a stricter true cut-set than MST-critical; independent of
  \texttt{mst\_critical\_only} (both may apply as successive intersections).
  Nested@$0.27$ majors $\le 1$ like MST; multi-seed A4-primary$+$bridge sample
  ARI never recovers.
  \item Soft-capacity cut-set (A2-T37 / A2-T39; flags default off):
  \texttt{soft\_capacity\_only} intersects hollow cuts with edges whose
  capacity score is at least
  \texttt{soft\_capacity\_frac}${}\cdot\max\mathrm{score}$ (operational
  $f{=}0.25$). Score method is selected by
  \texttt{HollowEdgeConfig.soft\_capacity\_method} (A2-T39):
  \texttt{"betweenness"} (default Brandes) or \texttt{"bridge\_mass"}
  (min-cut mass on graph bridges:
  $\min(|C_u|,|C_v|)$ after removing a bridge; non-bridges score $0$).
  Continuous capacity/flow proxy between hard bridges and unrestricted
  hollow; independent of MST/bridge flags (successive intersections).
  Nested@$0.27$ with A4-primary$+$soft majors $\le 1$ like MST/bridge;
  sample-ARI unrecovered under either method. Denser-scaffold
  ($n$/$\texttt{max\_nodes}$) probes collapse nests $K{=}2\!\to\!\le 1$ and
  do not restore ARI (A2-T36); denser$\times$soft (betweenness or
  bridge-mass) likewise leaves nested/tori sample-ARI unrecovered
  (A2-T39-followon) --- still proposal-path; do \emph{not} flip awaiting.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Soft-capacity frac sweep and soft$\times$persist\_agree (proposed;
A2-T40 / A3-T53).}
Module \texttt{proteus.stage1.edge\_evidence} exports
\texttt{format\_soft\_capacity\_frac\_sweep\_table} /
\texttt{SOFT\_CAPACITY\_FRAC\_SWEEP\_*} (A4-primary$+$betweenness; proposal-path
handoff --- not an acceptance rule):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Nested@$\tau{=}0.27$: majors $\le 1$ for every probed
  \texttt{soft\_capacity\_frac}${}\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9\}$ (A4-alone
  majors${=}2$, sample-ARI$\approx 0.12$). Soft capacity collapses the
  cut-set across the full frac range --- not a narrow $f{=}0.25$ artifact.
  \item Linked tori@$\tau{=}0.5$: majors${=}2$ with sample-ARI$\approx 0.26$
  (chance-level) for $f\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5\}$; at $f{=}0.9$ majors drop to
  $1$ (no ARI). Raising the capacity floor does not buy sample-ARI; it only
  eventually extinguishes the second major.
  \item Soft$\times$\texttt{hollow\_require\_persistent\_agree} leaf harness
  (A2-T40): uniforms (circle/swiss) stay $1$ leaf (safe); soft alone collapses
  A4-primary tori $K{=}2\!\to\!1$; soft$+$persist leaves nested/tori
  unrecovered ($1$ leaf). Conjunction is uniform-safe but not a recovery path.
\end{itemize}
Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip S14.3 soft-capacity knobs or awaiting
on this sweep.

\paragraph{Soft$\times$\texttt{require\_gabriel\_and\_h} conjunction (proposed;
A2-T41 / A3-T56 / A3-EXP-si59).}
Module \texttt{proteus.stage1.edge\_evidence} exports
\texttt{SOFT\_X\_GABRIEL\_CONJ\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_x\_gabriel\_conj\_table} (A4-primary$+$betweenness
$f{=}0.25$; successive intersections --- not an acceptance rule):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Soft alone: nested@$\tau{=}0.27$ majors $\le 1$; linked tori@$\tau{=}0.5$
  keeps $K{=}2$ with sample-ARI$\approx 0.26$ (chance-level).
  \item \texttt{require\_gabriel\_and\_h} alone and soft$\times$conj: both nested
  and tori collapse to majors $\le 1$.
\end{itemize}
Collapse $\neq$ sample-ARI recovery. Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip
S14.3 or awaiting.

\paragraph{Multi-seed soft-capacity frac sweep (proposed; A2-T42 /
A3-T56 / A3-EXP-si59).}
Export \texttt{SOFT\_CAPACITY\_FRAC\_MULTISEED\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_capacity\_frac\_multiseed\_table} extends the A2-T40
seed-$0$ frac table across scaffold seeds $0..2$ (A4-primary$+$betweenness):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Nested@$\tau{=}0.27$: majors $\le 1$ for every seed and every probed
  $f\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5,0.9\}$.
  \item Tori@$\tau{=}0.5$: chance-ARI $K{=}2$ is \emph{seed-fragile}
  (seed~$0$ until $f{=}0.9$; seed~$2$ only at $f{=}0.1$ with
  ARI$\approx 0.22$; seed~$1$ already $\le 1$).
\end{itemize}
No sample-ARI recovery; defaults off; do \emph{not} flip awaiting.

\paragraph{A4 primary \texttt{HollowEdgeConfig} handoff (proposed; A4-T24).}
ROC / sheet-null exporter
\texttt{recommend\_hollow\_edge\_configs} (tests harness; not S14.3 defaults)
recommends a \emph{primary} operating point for A2 apply-behind-flag work
(A2-T33):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Primary: $(f_{\mathrm{mid}},h_0,n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min},\mathrm{Gabriel})
  {=}(0.5,0.7,0.5,\mathrm{False})$ --- sheet FPR${=}0$, bridge TPR$\approx 0.9$,
  sheet $q_{01}(H)\approx 0.82$, AUC$\approx 0.999$.
  \item Alternates retain A2-parity $(0.35,0.35)$ with/without Gabriel and
  nearby $(0.35,0.25)$ / $(0.35,0.5)$ rows; prefer \emph{no}-Gabriel.
\end{itemize}
\textbf{Sheet-null safe $\neq$ nested cut-set recovery}: sample-ARI on nested /
tori at operational $\tau^\star$ remains the acceptance bar; $K{=}2$ alone is
insufficient. Do \emph{not} flip S14.3 $h_0$ / $f_{\mathrm{mid}}$ or awaiting
on this handoff --- production still defaults to the provisional
$(0.35,0.35)$ wiring behind the off prepass flag.

\paragraph{ROC mid-frac table and sheet-null vs $h_0$ tension (proposed;
A4-T18 continuation).}
Adversarial-null ROC on sheet$+$gap generators (A4; proposal-path evidence,
not an acceptance flip):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}0.25$: AUC $\approx 0.993$;
  $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}0.35$: AUC $\approx 1.0$;
  $f_{\mathrm{mid}}{=}0.5$: AUC $\approx 0.999$ (A4 primary mid).
  \item A2-parity operating point $(f_{\mathrm{mid}},h_0){=}(0.35,0.35)$ with
  Gabriel fallback: TPR${=}1$, FPR$\approx 0.014$ on the gap/tissue suite;
  no-Gabriel alt FPR$\approx 0.014$.
  \item Sheet null (connected manifold, no void): at mid $0.35$, mean
  $H\approx 1.03$, empirical $q_{01}(H)\approx 0.57$; at mid $0.5$,
  $q_{01}(H)\approx 0.82$ (A4 primary $h_0{=}0.7$ sits below that $q_{01}$).
\end{itemize}
Tension for calibration: A2's operational $h_0{=}0.35$ is below the sheet
null $q_{01}$ at mid $0.35$, so it is permissive relative to a $1\%$-tail
sheet null and relies on Gabriel / $n_{\mathrm{end}}^{\min}$ for low-count
edges. The A4 primary raises both mid and $h_0$ while dropping Gabriel ---
still proposal-path. Raising $h_0$ toward the sheet $q_{01}$ (or a proper
Poisson LR threshold) must be re-checked against TPR on gap bridges and
against nested/tori sample-ARI (not $K$ alone). Do not flip S14.3 $h_0$ or
awaiting on this table.

\paragraph{Poisson-null sheet $H$ export (proposed; A2-T38).}
Module \texttt{proteus.stage1.edge\_evidence} exports
\texttt{format\_poisson\_null\_h0\_table} / frozen
\texttt{POISSON\_NULL\_SHEET\_H\_QUANTILES} (adversarial sheet harness;
seed~$0$; proposal-path handoff --- not a Youden / LR acceptance rule):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item mid $0.25/0.35/0.5$: $q_{01}(H)\approx 0.15/0.43/0.76$ with
  mean $H\approx 1$ (export rounds; exact table rows in code).
  \item A4 primary mid${=}0.5$, $h_0{=}0.7$ remains $\le$ the recommend-harness
  sheet $q_{01}\approx 0.82$ (gabriel off; sheet FPR$\approx 0$, bridge
  TPR$\approx 0.9$).
\end{itemize}
Sheet-null safe $\neq$ nested cut-set / sample-ARI recovery. Do not flip
S14.3 $h_0$ or awaiting on this export.

\paragraph{Proposed Youden / Poisson-LR $h_0$ calibration (proposed; A2-T43 /
A3-T56 / A3-EXP-si60).}
Module \texttt{proteus.stage1.edge\_evidence} exports
\texttt{PROPOSED\_H0\_CALIBRATION\_*} /
\texttt{proposed\_h0\_calibrated\_config} (mid${=}0.5$, gabriel off;
proposal-path candidates --- \emph{never} the
\texttt{HollowEdgeConfig} / \texttt{RecursionConfig} default):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Youden max $J\approx 0.97$ at $h_0\approx 0.73$ (TPR${=}1$,
  FPR$\approx 0.03$) on the mid${=}0.5$ adversarial ROC.
  \item A4 primary $h_0{=}0.7$ (conservative Youden-family: sheet FPR$\approx 0$,
  bridge TPR$\approx 0.9$).
  \item Poisson-LR / sheet-null lower-tail snap: mid${=}0.5$
  $q_{01}(H)\approx 0.76$ $\Rightarrow$ proposed $h_0{=}0.76$.
  \item Operational default $h_0{=}0.35$ is weak on Youden relative to these
  candidates.
\end{itemize}
Sheet/bridge ROC safe $\neq$ nested/tori sample-ARI recovery. Nested/tori
remain unrecovered under the proposed configs. Do \emph{not} flip S14.3
$h_0$ / $f_{\mathrm{mid}}$ or awaiting.

\paragraph{Soft$\times$proposed $h_0$ and denser$\times$proposed (proposed;
A2-T44-followon / A3-EXP-si63).}
Export tables \texttt{SOFT\_X\_PROPOSED\_H0\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_x\_proposed\_h0\_table} and
\texttt{DENSER\_PROPOSED\_H0\_*} /
\texttt{format\_denser\_proposed\_h0\_table} (mid${=}0.5$, gabriel off,
betweenness $f{=}0.25$; proposal-path --- not an acceptance rule):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Baseline scaffold: Youden / Youden-A4 / Poisson-LR alone keep nested
  $+$ tori at chance-ARI $K{=}2$ (nested ARI$\approx 0.12/0.08$; tori
  ARI$\approx 0.26$). Soft$\times$any proposed $h_0$ collapses nested majors
  $\le 1$ but retains tori $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.26$.
  \item Denser scaffold ($n{=}160/240$, \texttt{max\_nodes}${=}128$): Youden
  alone collapses nested $\le 1$ while tori stays $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.14$;
  soft$\times$Youden / soft$\times$Poisson-LR collapse \emph{both} to
  $\le 1$ major.
\end{itemize}
Soft capacity is the collapse driver; calibrated $h_0$ alone does not unlock
sample-ARI recovery. Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip S14.3 or awaiting.

\paragraph{Multi-seed soft$\times$Youden and soft$\times h_0$ near-null contrast
(proposed; A2-T44..T46 / A3-T59).}
Exports \texttt{SOFT\_X\_YOUDEN\_MULTISEED\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_x\_youden\_multiseed\_table} and
\texttt{SOFT\_H0\_METHOD\_CONTRAST\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_h0\_method\_contrast\_table} (mid${=}0.5$, gabriel off,
betweenness $f{=}0.25$; proposal-path --- not an acceptance rule):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Multi-seed soft$\times$Youden $h_0\approx 0.73$ (seeds $0$--$2$):
  seed~$0$ soft collapses nested $\le 1$ / keeps tori $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.26$;
  seed~$1$ soft \emph{inflates} nested spurious $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.08$
  while Youden alone stayed $\le 1$; seed~$2$ soft collapses both $\le 1$
  (A2-T44). Soft$\times$Youden is seed-fragile.
  \item Soft$\times h_0$ method contrast (A2-T46): soft$\times$Youden /
  soft$\times$A4-primary / soft$\times$Poisson-LR are
  \emph{identical} on seed~$0$ (nested $\le 1$, tori $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.26$)
  for $h_0\in\{0.7,0.73,0.76\}$ --- $h_0$ choice is near-null under soft;
  soft dominates majors/ARI.
  \item Denser$\times$proposed (A2-T45): Youden alone denser tori
  ARI$\approx 0.14$; soft$\times*$ collapses both scaffolds $\le 1$.
\end{itemize}
Calibrated $h_0$ alone / under soft does not unlock sample-ARI recovery.
Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip S14.3 or awaiting.

\paragraph{Soft-frac$\times$Youden seed-$1$ inflate window and denser kill
(proposed; A2-T47..T49 / A3-T62).}
Exports \texttt{SOFT\_FRAC\_X\_YOUDEN\_SEED\_INFLATE\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_frac\_x\_youden\_seed\_inflate\_table} and
\texttt{DENSER\_SOFT\_X\_YOUDEN\_MULTISEED\_*} /
\texttt{format\_denser\_soft\_x\_youden\_multiseed\_table}
(mid${=}0.5$, gabriel off, betweenness; proposal-path):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Seed-$1$ nested inflate is \emph{frac-windowed} (A2-T47): soft
  alone at $f\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5\}$ yields nested spurious $K{=}2$
  ARI$\approx 0.05$--$0.08$ while Youden alone stays $\le 1$; $f\ge 0.75$
  collapses nested $\le 1$. Seed~$0$ soft collapses nested at all fracs
  (tori $K{=}2$ until $f\ge 0.75$); seed~$2$ never inflates.
  \item Denser scaffold ($n{=}160/240$, \texttt{max\_nodes}${=}128$;
  A2-T48): soft$\times$Youden collapses both majors $\le 1$ on seeds
  $0$--$2$; baseline seed-$1$ inflate is \emph{absent} on denser.
  \item Denser $h_0$-only contrast (A2-T49): seed~$0$ Youden alone keeps
  tori $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.14$ while soft$\times*$ is stricter
  (both $\le 1$); seeds $1$--$2$ both modes $\le 1$. Soft$\neq$sample-ARI.
\end{itemize}
Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip S14.3 or awaiting.

\paragraph{Denser soft-frac window kill, bridge\_mass contrast, and
operational $\tau^\star$ (proposed; A2-T50..T52 / A3-EXP-si65).}
Exports \texttt{DENSER\_SOFT\_FRAC\_X\_YOUDEN\_SEED\_INFLATE\_*} /
\texttt{format\_denser\_soft\_frac\_x\_youden\_seed\_inflate\_table},
\texttt{BRIDGE\_MASS\_X\_YOUDEN\_SEED\_INFLATE\_*} /
\texttt{format\_bridge\_mass\_x\_youden\_seed\_inflate\_table}, and
\texttt{SOFT\_X\_YOUDEN\_TAU\_STAR\_*} /
\texttt{format\_soft\_x\_youden\_tau\_star\_table}
(mid${=}0.5$, gabriel off; proposal-path):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Denser soft\_frac$\times$Youden seed-$1$ window (A2-T50;
  $n{=}160/240$, \texttt{max\_nodes}${=}128$): across
  $f\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9\}$ denser seed~$1$ never inflates
  (nested$+$tori $\le 1$) --- denser kills the baseline frac-window.
  Seed~$0$ soft$_{0.1}$ keeps tori $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.18$; soft
  $\ge 0.25$ collapses; seed~$2$ all $\le 1$.
  \item Bridge\_mass vs betweenness (A2-T51; baseline $n{=}80/120$):
  seed-$1$ betweenness inflate is \emph{method-specific}
  ($f\in\{0.1,0.25,0.5\}\to$ nested $K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.05$--$0.08$);
  \texttt{bridge\_mass} never inflates seed~$1$ across the frac window.
  Multi-seed@$f{=}0.25$: seed~$0$ soft\_bet keeps tori $K{=}2$ /
  soft\_mass collapses; seed~$2$ both soft $\le 1$.
  \item Soft$\times$Youden at operational scale-search $\tau^\star$
  (A2-T52; lean \texttt{max\_grid\_points}${=}12$): seed-$1$ probe-tau
  soft inflate is \emph{absent} at $\tau^\star$ (nested$+$tori $\le 1$);
  seed~$0$ tori keeps chance-ARI $K\ge 2$ under Youden ($K{=}3$
  ARI$\approx 0.22$) and soft ($K{=}2$ ARI$\approx 0.27$); seeds
  $1$--$2$ both $\le 1$.
\end{itemize}
Seed-$1$ inflate is baseline$+$betweenness$+$probe-tau only; denser /
\texttt{bridge\_mass} / operational $\tau^\star$ all kill it.
Soft$\neq$sample-ARI. Defaults remain off; do \emph{not} flip S14.3 or
awaiting.

\paragraph{Multi-$\tau$ ROC confirmation of the A4 primary (proposed; A4-T27).}
Multi-$\tau$ sheet$+$gap ROC (A4-T27) keeps the primary
$(f_{\mathrm{mid}},h_0,\mathrm{Gabriel}){=}(0.5,0.7,\mathrm{False})$ as the
preferred operating point at density~$1$: FPR${=}0$, TPR$\approx 0.9$,
AUC$\approx 0.999$. Raising mid above $0.5$ (e.g.\ $0.6/0.7$) collapses TPR;
thinning the sheet (density $0.5$) raises FPR (A4 primary $0\to\approx 0.058$
--$0.087$; A2-parity $(0.35,0.35)$ $0.014\to\approx 0.174$). Still
proposal-path --- do not flip S14.3 or awaiting.

Do not retire the radial/PCA family or flip awaiting until calibrated $h_0$ $+$
$\tau$-coupling evidence lands with sample-ARI recovery. Supporting draft:
\texttt{docs/Proteus/paper\_1\_foundational/reference/empty\_region\_evidence\_and\_scale.md}.

\subsection*{S2.6.3 Dirichlet--Multinomial Cluster-Acceptance Reduction (proposed)}
\label{sec:si-dm-cluster-reduction}
S2.6.1 identifies the Stage-2 Dirichlet--multinomial evidence gate (S3.4) as the
complementary non-degenerate likelihood-ratio null for cluster acceptance, but
S3.4 is written for \emph{node/star topology edits} and does not state how a
Stage-1 partition-into-$K$ decision reduces to an $F_{\mathrm{DM}}$ comparison.
This subsection specifies that reduction. It is \textbf{operational / proposed}
(default off, implemented as \texttt{stage1.dm\_cluster} behind
\texttt{RecursionConfig.require\_dm\_split}); it is validated below but does not
yet license deleting the S2.6.1 stand-ins.

\paragraph{Block-model homogeneity test.} A candidate partition of a region into
blocks $C_1,\dots,C_K$ induces a directed block-flow matrix
\[
N_{k\ell}=\sum_{a\in C_k}\sum_{b\in C_\ell} n_{a\to b}
\]
from the Hebbian transition counts (all tiers, per S2.3.3/S3.4). The question
``one feature or $K$?'' is then a Dirichlet--multinomial test of homogeneity of
the $K$ routing rows over a fixed $K$-outcome space (the blocks): the
one-feature \emph{keep} model scores the pooled row $\sum_k N_{k\cdot}$ under a
single $\mathrm{DM}(J{=}K,\alpha_0)$ marginal, while the \emph{split} model
scores each row $N_{k\cdot}$ separately. By the standard contingency Bayes
factor this is exactly the S3.4 acceptance rule $F_{\mathrm{DM}}(M_{\mathrm{edit}})
< F_{\mathrm{DM}}(M_{\mathrm{keep}}) - \log\tau_{\mathrm{BF}}$ with
\[
\log \mathrm{BF}
=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\log m\!\left(N_{k\cdot}\right)-\log m\!\left(\textstyle\sum_k N_{k\cdot}\right),
\qquad
m(\cdot)=\text{$\mathrm{DM}$ marginal of Eq.~\eqref{eq:si-fdm}},
\]
and the BDeu concentration $\alpha_0=1/(d_{\mathrm{final}}+1)$ of S2.7. For
$K{=}2$ this coincides term-for-term with the two-subpopulation gate of S3.5,
and the one-feature null is \emph{non-degenerate} (unlike $Q(\mathcal P;v)\to+\infty$).
An agglomerative variant (merge the adjacent block pair with the lowest pairwise
$\log\mathrm{BF}$, recomputing $N$ each step until every remaining adjacent split
clears $\log\tau_{\mathrm{BF}}$) replaces the S2.6.1 cleanup passes with a single
evidence criterion.

\paragraph{Finding: single-scale DM inherits the S2.6.1 under-determination.}
Applied at a \emph{single} scale, and run through the full recursion, this
reduction \emph{over-fragments at every level}: a uniform ring yields tens of
leaves ($\sim 56$), a Swiss roll $\sim 76$, and even the genuinely $6$-leaf
hierarchical Gaussian fragments to $\sim 32$ because each recovered blob is
itself re-split by the single-scale gate. The arcs of a developable manifold
have band-concentrated block rows (each arc routes to itself and its two
neighbors) that the homogeneity test reads as heterogeneous. This is the
same single-scale limitation as $Q$ (S2.6.1): the intrinsic-vs-composite
distinction is a cross-scale statement, and no single-snapshot statistic --- $Q$,
conductance, or block-model $\log\mathrm{BF}$ --- separates a uniform arc-partition
from a real multi-modal one. The DM margin's discriminating power is therefore
\emph{complementary} to, not a replacement for, the persistence signal.

\paragraph{Composition with persistence.} Combining the DM reduction with the
S2.6.2 coarse-anchored persistence gate (\texttt{require\_persistent\_split} $\wedge$
\texttt{require\_dm\_split}) recovers all three canonical scenarios --- circle
$\to 1$, hierarchical Gaussian $\to 6$, Swiss roll $\to 1$ --- with the
within-region partition produced by the DM-gated merge rather than the S2.6.1
stand-ins: persistence rejects the uniform-manifold arc-partitions cross-scale,
and the DM Bayes factor supplies the heuristic-free within-region merge and the
principled acceptance margin the persistence overlap threshold lacked. This
matches the default (stand-in) leaf counts on all three scenarios. Deletion of
the stand-ins remains gated on the fuller scenario suite (nested spheres, linked
tori, manifold zoo) and the acceptance-path audit (OPEN\_ISSUES~\#27); the
reduction is retained as an operational, default-off path until then.

\subsection*{S2.7 Dirichlet Concentration from the BDeu Prior}
\label{sec:si-bdeu-prior}
Let $J_i$ be the effective number of outgoing outcomes for node $i$, approximated by the local branching factor $d_{\mathrm{final},i}+1$. The BDeu convention assigns one equivalent pseudo-observation spread uniformly over these outcomes:
\[
\alpha_{0,i}=\frac{1}{d_{\mathrm{final},i}+1}.
\label{eq:si-bdeu-alpha}
\]
This concentration is used consistently in transition smoothing, AP self-preference, and the Dirichlet--multinomial evidence gate. The point is not to tune a separate smoothing knob for every dimension; the same dimension-aware prior regularizes zero counts and supplies the Occam factor in structure selection.

\subsection*{S2.8 Resolution Theory}
\label{sec:si-resolution-theory}
The graph-local Q-score of S2.6 is the algorithmic separability criterion. This
subsection states the corresponding information-theoretic preconditions under
which the cluster definition can be satisfied at all. Throughout, $\tau$ denotes
the Gaussian smoothing bandwidth acting on the latent density; the
\hyperref[sec:si-tau-heat-bridge]{variance-cap / heat-kernel bridge of S2.5.4}
licenses identifying it with the operational variance cap under the isotropic
convention $\Sigma_{\mathrm{smooth}}=\tau I$.

\paragraph{Density model.}
At fixed local effective dimension $d$, model the density as
\[
\rho(x)=u+\sum_{i=1}^m H_i
\exp\!\left(-\frac{\|x-\mu_i\|^2}{2\sigma_i^2}\right),
\]
where $u>0$ is the local tissue floor, $H_i$ is the peak excess above tissue,
$\sigma_i$ is the intrinsic width, and $\mu_i$ is the mode center. Under
Gaussian smoothing by variance $\tau$, a single mode evolves as
\[
\rho_\tau(x)=
u+
H_i\left(\frac{\sigma_i^2}{\sigma_i^2+\tau}\right)^{d/2}
\exp\!\left(
-\frac{\|x-\mu_i\|^2}{2(\sigma_i^2+\tau)}
\right).
\]
The smoothed width and peak excess are therefore
\[
s_i(\tau)=\sqrt{\sigma_i^2+\tau},
\qquad
h_i(\tau)=
H_i\left(\frac{\sigma_i^2}{\sigma_i^2+\tau}\right)^{d/2}.
\]

\paragraph{Detectability above tissue.}
Let $u$ denote the local floor relevant to the current recursion context. Define
the scale-dependent signal-to-tissue contrast
\[
C_i(\tau):=\frac{h_i(\tau)}{u}
=
\frac{H_i}{u}
\left(\frac{\sigma_i^2}{\sigma_i^2+\tau}\right)^{d/2}.
\]
For a mode to satisfy the coherence part of the cluster definition, it must
retain enough excess above this floor:
\[
C_i(\tau)\ge\kappa_{\mathrm{det}}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
u\le
\frac{H_i}{\kappa_{\mathrm{det}}}
\left(\frac{\sigma_i^2}{\sigma_i^2+\tau}\right)^{d/2},
\]
and the maximum admissible operating scale is
\[
\tau_{\mathrm{det},i}
:=
\sigma_i^2
\left[
\left(\frac{H_i}{\kappa_{\mathrm{det}}\,u}\right)^{2/d}-1
\right].
\]
At the natural operating scale $\tau=\sigma_i^2$, this becomes the
characteristic-scale detectability condition
\[
\frac{H_i}{u}\ge \kappa_{\mathrm{det}}\,2^{d/2}.
\]

\paragraph{Clean resolvability of two modes.}
Two equal-width isotropic Gaussian modes of common width $\sigma$, separated by
$\Delta$, remain bimodal after smoothing by variance $\tau$ if and only if
\[
\Delta>2\sqrt{\sigma^2+\tau}.
\]
The associated maximum separation-preserving operating scale is
\[
\tau_{\mathrm{sep}}=\frac{\Delta^2}{4}-\sigma^2.
\]
At the natural operating scale $\tau=\sigma^2$, the characteristic-scale
threshold is
\[
\Delta>2\sqrt{2}\,\sigma.
\]
For unequal widths, a conservative sufficient condition is
\[
\Delta>
2\max\!\bigl(\sqrt{\sigma_a^2+\tau},\,\sqrt{\sigma_b^2+\tau}\bigr).
\]

\paragraph{Bridge to the graph-local Q criterion.}
If two clusters are locally Gaussian with empirical scales $\hat v_A,\hat v_B$
and centroid separation $\Delta_{AB}$, their all-pairs inter-cluster coupling
decays approximately as
\[
\mathrm{Inter}(A,B;v)
\approx
\exp\!\left(
-\frac{\Delta_{AB}^2}
{2\,d_{\mathrm{eff}}(v+\hat v_A+\hat v_B)}
\right).
\]
Clean graph-level separation requires this inter-cluster term to be much
smaller than typical intra-cluster evidence, yielding the heuristic condition
\[
\Delta_{AB}^2\gg d_{\mathrm{eff}}(v+\hat v_A+\hat v_B).
\]
At equilibrium, where $v\approx\hat v_A\approx\hat v_B\approx\sigma^2$, this
becomes
\[
\Delta_{AB}^2\gg 3\,d_{\mathrm{eff}}\,\sigma^2.
\]
The continuous bimodality bound is therefore the conservative outer envelope,
while the Q-gated scaffold may sometimes resolve slightly tighter pairs because
it also uses co-activation evidence. The gap between the continuous bound and
the graph-local bound is the operating margin.

\section*{S3. Structure Selection and Statistical Gates}

\subsection*{S3.1 Link Pruning}
\label{sec:si-link-pruning}
The pruning primitive is the directed \emph{co-activation transition mass} $C(i\!\to\! j)=n_{i\to j}$: how often local routing from node $i$ selects neighbor $j$ as a co-activated near-neighbor. This is the quantity that separates a meaningful adjacency from an incidental high-dimensional Delaunay sliver---a genuine manifold edge carries a substantial share of a node's outgoing routing traffic, whereas a sliver carries a vanishing share that shrinks as ambient dimension grows.

\paragraph{Directed floor with bilateral agreement (default).}
For each source node $i$ with total outgoing mass $T_i=\sum_j C(i\!\to\! j)$, define the directed floor
\[
m_{\mathrm{floor},i}=\frac{\beta_{\mathrm{prune}}}{2\max(D,1)}\,T_i,
\qquad \beta_{\mathrm{prune}}=0.5,
\label{eq:si-directed-floor}
\]
where $D$ is the working intrinsic dimension of node $i$'s neighborhood. The factor $2D$ is the number of notional adjacency slots a $D$-dimensional cell occupies (two per axis): under a uniform split of $T_i$ across $2D$ slots each slot carries $T_i/(2D)$, so $m_{\mathrm{floor},i}$ keeps edges whose directed share is at least $\beta_{\mathrm{prune}}$ times that reference slot mass. Source $i$ votes to prune edge $\{i,j\}$ when $C(i\!\to\! j)<m_{\mathrm{floor},i}$. An edge is deleted only under \emph{bilateral agreement}: both endpoints must vote to prune. This keeps the primitive symmetric on the undirected graph while the evidence itself stays directed.

\paragraph{Neonatal protection.}
A newly created or newly lifted edge is protected from removal until its age exceeds a neonatal guard ($\texttt{protected\_until}$). It still contributes to the outgoing totals $T_i$ used to compute floors, but it cannot itself be deleted while protected. This prevents transient low counts from deleting a bridge before it has had a fair chance to accumulate evidence.

\paragraph{Post-clustering lifted-edge demotion.}
After clustering (S2.6.1), the same floor rule is applied to the \emph{lifted} edges only, with $D$ taken as the per-cluster median $d_{\mathrm{final}}$; lifted edges failing the floor bilaterally are \emph{demoted} to shadow status rather than deleted, so their low-grade adjacency evidence is retained for later stages.

\paragraph{Wilson-interval alternative.}
An alternative gauntlet replaces the floor test with a one-sided Wilson upper bound on the link's relative transition proportion $p$ from count $n$,
\[
\nu^+
=
\frac{
p+z^2/(2n)+z\sqrt{p(1-p)/n+z^2/(4n^2)}
}{
1+z^2/n
},
\qquad z=1.2816
\label{eq:si-wilson-link}
\]
(a 90\% one-sided comparison), protecting a new link until $n_{\mathrm{eff}}\ge n_{\mathrm{req}}$ from an analytic power calculation, then voting to prune when $\nu^+$ falls below the median peer significance in the local star, again with bilateral agreement. The Wilson rule is retained as a statistical alternative; the directed floor is the operational default because it is scale-free in $T_i$ and needs no per-link power calculation.

\subsection*{S3.2 Node Pruning}
A node is a prune candidate only if it is both statistically weak and geometrically unneeded. The default rule combines:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item neighborhood stability, using a Welch test on local density/response estimates;
  \item an arrival-rate fairness test $\lambda_{\mathrm{est}}=h_i/\mathrm{lifetime}\ge 2/\delta_{\min}$;
  \item low relative hit mass, $h_i<0.5$ times the neighbor mean;
  \item low local variance, $\sigma_i^2<0.5\,\tau_{\mathrm{local},i}$.
\end{itemize}
Low hits alone are not sufficient: a low-mass node in a high-variance or curved region may be structurally important and should not be removed until the region has stabilized.

\subsection*{S3.3 Stage 2 Prunes and Merges}
Stage~2 node deletions are arbitrated by the node's simplex star. A deletion proposal is vetoed if removing the node creates a large local reconstruction error, destabilizes adjacent stars, or violates the local reassignment radius bound. Prune candidates inherit $h_j<0.5\bar h$ and $\sigma_j^2<0.5\,\tau_{\mathrm{local},j}$ from S3.2 and additionally require
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{prune}}
:=
\max_{x\in V_j}\min_{s\ne j}\|x-w_s\|
\le C_Q(d)\sqrt{\tau_{\mathrm{local},j}},
\]
where $C_Q(d)$ is the variance-cap star-radius constant in the regular interior. Merge proposals $\{a,b\}\mapsto m$ require, before evidence scoring,
\[
\|w_a-w_b\|\le\rho_{\mathrm{merge}}\sqrt{\tau_{ab}},
\qquad
P(V_a\cup V_b)\le p_{\mathrm{merge}},
\qquad
\max(\sigma_a^2,\sigma_b^2)\le v_{\mathrm{merge}}\tau_{ab},
\]
with defaults
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{merge}}\le 2C_Q(d),\qquad
p_{\mathrm{merge}}\le \bar h/N,\qquad
v_{\mathrm{merge}}\le 1,\qquad
\tau_{ab}=\max(\tau_{\mathrm{local},a},\tau_{\mathrm{local},b}).
\]
Under these guards, the per-edit positive vertex-potential jump is bounded by
\[
\Delta L_{\mathrm{vertex}}^{\mathrm{prune/merge}}
\le
B_p
=
\max\!\left\{
p_{\mathrm{prune}}\bigl(\rho_{\mathrm{prune}}^2
+2\rho_{\mathrm{prune}}\sqrt{v_{\mathrm{prune}}\tau_*}\bigr),
p_{\mathrm{merge}}\bigl(\rho_{\mathrm{merge}}^2
+2\rho_{\mathrm{merge}}\sqrt{v_{\mathrm{merge}}\tau_*}\bigr)
\right\},
\label{eq:si-bp-jump}
\]
with $\tau_*=\max_i\tau_{\mathrm{local},i}$ and global bounded-support fallback $B_p\le R^2$.

\paragraph{Interior star-radius constant $C_Q(d)$.}
\label{par:si-cq-calibration}
$C_Q(d)$ is the typical worst-case reassignment distance of a cap-equilibrated
cell in the regular interior, normalized by $\sqrt\tau$. Writing
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{prune},j}=\max_{x\in V_j}\min_{s\ne j}\lVert x-w_s\rVert
\]
for the deletion-reassignment radius of node $j$ (the farthest a point owned by
$j$ must travel to the nearest surviving node if $j$ is removed), $C_Q(d)$ is the
median of $\rho_{\mathrm{prune},j}/\sqrt{\tau}$ over the regular interior. It is a
\emph{calibrated} constant (S14.3), measured on the same uniform $d$-ball
ensemble as $c_{d,k}$ (S2.5.5) --- the star radius rather than the $k$-NN radius
on identical equilibrated scaffolds. For each dimension: run the fixed-$\tau$
scaffold to equilibrium; assign each reference point to its nearest node; set
$\rho_{\mathrm{prune},j}$ to the largest second-nearest-node distance among the
points node $j$ owns; and record the median of
$\rho_{\mathrm{prune},j}/\sqrt\tau$ over the inner half of mature nodes by
distance from the ball center. The interior restriction excludes boundary cells
whose star radius is inflated by the missing exterior neighbors, and the inner
fraction (rather than an absolute radius) adapts to dimension, since in high $d$
most nodes concentrate near the surface. Repeat over independent ensembles and
take the median of the per-scaffold medians. The reference implementation is
\texttt{proteus.stage1.calibration.calibrate\_cq}; the shipped table is
\texttt{CQ\_TABLE}, regenerable via \texttt{python -m proteus.stage1.calibration}.

The worst-case reassignment distance is achieved near the deleted node's
centroid, where the nearest \emph{surviving} node sits one inter-node spacing
$s$ away; so $C_Q(d)$ tracks the equilibrium spacing, not the cell radius. Under
a locally uniform density the $k$-NN radius and the $1$-NN spacing are related by
$r_{k}=s\,(k/V_d)^{1/d}$, hence $s=r_{k}\,(V_d/k)^{1/d}$; substituting the
calibrated $r_{k}=c_{d,k}\sqrt\tau$ (S2.5.5) gives the anchor
\[
C_Q^{\mathrm{knn}}(d)=c_{d,k}\Bigl(\tfrac{V_d}{k}\Bigr)^{1/d},
\qquad V_d=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(d/2+1)},
\label{eq:si-cq-anchor}
\]
used as the runtime fallback \texttt{c\_q\_analytic} for dimensions outside the
tabulated grid. Using the \emph{calibrated} $c_{d,k}$ (not its analytic form) is
what makes this track the measured table: the equilibrium catchment variance
sits below the cap, which a bare cell-radius argument $R=\sqrt{(d+2)/d}\,\sqrt\tau$
would miss (it both conflates the spacing with the cell radius and assumes a
cap-saturated cell). The shipped table (uniform $d$-ball ensemble, $n=3000$
samples, four ensembles, $N_\star=120$, $k=8$, interior fraction $0.5$;
\texttt{CQ\_CALIBRATION\_META}) is
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
d & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4\\
\hline
C_Q(d) & 2.35 & 1.66 & 1.49 & 1.44\\
C_Q^{\mathrm{knn}}(d) & 2.43 & 1.78 & 1.30 & 1.17\\
\end{array}
\]
The anchor brackets the calibrated values within $\pm25\%$ across $d=1$--$4$,
confirming that the interior star radius scales as the inter-node spacing.

\subsection*{S3.4 Dirichlet--Multinomial Evidence Score}
\label{sec:si-dm-evidence}
For candidate topology $M$, define the affected node set $\mathcal V_{\mathrm{aff}}$ as the nodes whose stars or neighbor sets differ between the candidate and keep-topology baselines, plus a neighbor ring. Let $J_i(M)$ be the number of outgoing categorical outcomes at node $i$ under $M$. With BDeu concentration $\alpha_{0,i}$, the node-local Dirichlet--multinomial marginal is
\[
p_i(M)=
\frac{\Gamma(J_i\alpha_{0,i})}{\Gamma(J_i\alpha_{0,i}+n_i)}
\prod_{j=1}^{J_i}
\frac{\Gamma(\alpha_{0,i}+n_{i\to j})}{\Gamma(\alpha_{0,i})}.
\]
The affected-region score is
\[
F_{\mathrm{DM}}(\mathcal R;M)=-\sum_{i\in\mathcal V_{\mathrm{aff}}}\log p_i(M).
\label{eq:si-fdm}
\]
A candidate is accepted only if
\[
F_{\mathrm{DM}}(\mathcal R;M_{\mathrm{edit}})
<
F_{\mathrm{DM}}(\mathcal R;M_{\mathrm{keep}})-\log \tau_{\mathrm{BF}},
\]
where $\log \tau_{\mathrm{BF}}$ is the log-Bayes-factor margin. Candidate-dependent preprocessing is limited to deterministic rerouting of affected events under the candidate topology.

\subsection*{S3.5 Evidence Consistency}
For any finite candidate set $\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}$, assume the induced routers are identifiable and regular, routed transition events are i.i.d.\ or sufficiently mixing, probabilities are clipped below by $q_{\min}>0$ for finite-sample bounds, and the best candidate has positive expected log-likelihood margin $\Delta_{\mathcal R}>0$. Then the Dirichlet--multinomial marginal selects the best candidate with probability tending to one as the affected-region event count grows. The likelihood margin scales as $O(H_{\mathcal R}\Delta_{\mathcal R})$, while the BIC/Occam and fixed-threshold terms grow only logarithmically or remain constant.

\subsection*{S3.6 Gate Cadence and Edit Budgets}
\label{sec:si-gate-cadence}
Proposals are appended to a per-region queue $\mathcal Q_C$ keyed by diagnostic strength. The gate fires after an equilibration window
\[
W=\max\!\left(N_{\mathrm{nodes}}^{(C)},\,4k\,|\mathcal Q_C|\right)
\label{eq:si-gate-window}
\]
samples, which gives each affected node several updates before scoring; the window terminates early if $\mathcal Q_C$ is empty. Disjoint proposals may be scored in parallel. Overlapping proposals are processed in priority order, and proposals whose affected region overlaps an accepted edit are marked stale and re-evaluated against the post-edit topology.

Accepted edits trigger a short local settle of order $|\mathcal R|\,k$ updates. Hysteresis prevents immediate reversals: an edit cannot be reverted within
\[
T_{\mathrm{hyst}}=2W
\]
samples of acceptance. A per-level edit budget
\[
N_{\mathrm{edit}}^+(C)\le
\frac{N_{\mathrm{nodes}}^{(C)}}{\log N_{\mathrm{nodes}}^{(C)}}
\label{eq:si-edit-budget}
\]
accepted prune/merge edits per training epoch bounds the cumulative positive jump:
\[
A_{\mathrm{epoch}}^{(C)}
\le
B_p\,N_{\mathrm{edit}}^+(C)
\le
B_p\,\frac{N_{\mathrm{nodes}}^{(C)}}{\log N_{\mathrm{nodes}}^{(C)}}.
\]
For finite-epoch training, $A_T<\infty$ over each epoch. Indefinite streaming is treated as an engineering extension whose edit-rate tolerance is monitored rather than claimed as a first-paper guarantee.

\section*{S4. Stage 2 Complex Construction}

\subsection*{S4.1 Flag-Complex Invariant}
For a fixed region $C$, Stage~2 represents topology as the top-dimensional flag complex of the current \emph{lifted} Hebbian graph (S2.3.3). A $d$-simplex $\sigma=\{v_0,\dots,v_d\}$ is present exactly when every pair of vertices in $\sigma$ is a lifted Hebbian edge and the local intrinsic-dimension rule permits a $d$-simplex in that star. Node and link edits therefore induce simplex edits automatically: deleting a link deletes every simplex containing that link, and deleting a node deletes its full star. Geometric motion alone does not change simplex membership.

This invariant avoids separate Delaunay or alpha-complex maintenance. The graph owns topology; the complex is the closure of that topology at the locally estimated dimension. Geometry-dependent caches are recomputed only when the affected simplex geometry changes.

\subsection*{S4.2 Greedy Chaining Initialization}
Greedy Chaining initializes the flag complex from the stabilized Stage~1 graph. For each seed node, inspect rank-ordered neighbor subsets at the local intrinsic dimension, verify clique completion with adjacency hash lookups, and insert the canonical sorted simplex into a deduplication set. A breadth-first traversal over the graph amortizes the search because many candidate simplices are encountered from multiple seeds.

For heterogeneous intrinsic dimension, node $i$ enumerates subsets sized by $d_{\mathrm{final},i}$. Cross-junction candidates that do not form valid cliques simply fail verification; the resulting incidence-count distribution is itself a useful junction diagnostic. Nodes with zero simplex incidence after the main pass receive one final node-seeded extension attempt. Remaining orphans are deferred to continuous simplex discovery when later Hebbian links complete valid cliques.

\subsection*{S4.3 Flow-Conserving Split Initialization}
Flow conservation is the special case of the refinement-admissibility principle (S2.3.1) for sufficient statistics of partition-marginal flux: hit counts and transition tallies decompose exactly under refinement because the catchment mass partitions, so the children's totals must sum to the parent's.

When a split inserts a new vertex $k$ into a parent simplex or node star, transition tallies should be initialized so the local flow field is not artificially reset. If $C$ is a parent simplex with vertices $V(C)$ and $h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j)$ denotes pre-split internal hit counts, initialize new tallies by distributing the old internal flow through the new vertex:
\[
h_{\mathrm{new}}(k,v_i)
=\frac12\sum_{\substack{v_j\in V(C)\\j\ne i}}h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j).
\]
Simultaneously, original internal edges are halved: $h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j)\to\tfrac12\,h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j)$ for all $v_i,v_j\in V(C)$ with $v_i,v_j\ne k$. Under this convention total internal mass is preserved exactly:
\[
\sum_i h_{\mathrm{new}}(k,v_i)
+\!\!\sum_{\substack{i<j\\v_i,v_j\ne k}}\tfrac12\,h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j)
=\sum_{i<j}h_{\mathrm{old}}(v_i,v_j).
\]
The rule matters for dynamic stability: accepted edits should perturb routing statistics by a bounded amount rather than erasing local evidence.

\subsection*{S4.4 Transition Counts Across Levels and Stages}
\label{sec:si-transition-policies}
There are four warm-start regimes, each an instance of the refinement-admissibility principle of S2.3.1.
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item[\emph{T0.}] \emph{Within-region split.} A variance-cap split along $\mathbf u_i$ produces two children from one node. Each child inherits the shadow pair corresponding to its side of the split plane (S2.3.2), with a Steiner parallel-axis correction so the inherited moments are referenced to the child's new position rather than the parent's old origin. Both children reset their own shadow pairs, deferred nudge $\mathbf a_i$, and EWMA precision counter to zero, and inherit $\mathbf u_i$ and $d_{\mathrm{final},i}$. Hit counts distribute by shadow masses $h_i^\pm$, satisfying hard-Voronoi conservation. Transition counts on incident links are halved per S4.3. If shadows are not maintained, both children reset all frame-relative state to the EWMA prior (zero) and split hit counts evenly.
  \item \emph{T1: cross-grid within one recursion level.} Adjacent scale-grid evaluations preserve topology and neighbor sets. Hit counts $h_i$ and transition counts $n_{i\to j}$ are inherited unchanged; EWMA moments $(\mathbf m_i,\mathbf s_i)$ are rescaled or attenuated using the same default shrink $\gamma_0\approx0.7$ used at Stage~2 entry; the nudge accumulator $\mathbf a_i$ is reset to zero.
  \item \emph{T2: parent-to-child recursion.} A child region restricts the neighbor set and sample support, then applies the S1.4 intrinsic-dimension PCA transfer in the active parent frame. Child samples and inherited node positions are projected into the child PCA frame, $D_{\mathrm{subspace}}$ is set to the retained child rank, and inherited $d_{\mathrm{final}}$ values carry forward as the dimension seeds for the child. Hit counts, transition counts, EWMA moments, and nudges are reset to zero. This restores the BDeu/DM interpretation under the child's restricted router.
  \item \emph{T3: Stage~1 to Stage~2.} Stage~2 inherits stabilized node positions, links, hit counts, and transition counts. EWMA moments are shrunk by $\gamma_0\approx0.7$, and the nudge accumulator is reset to zero.
\end{enumerate}
These policies are part of the statistical specification, not implementation details: they determine when counts remain evidence for the same router and when they must be re-measured.

\subsection*{S4.5 Stage 2 Data Structures}
Each simplex maintains the small geometry and tally caches needed by later stages: vertex IDs, facet IDs, volume $|S|_d$, face normals, pairwise edge norms, face-pressure tallies (\texttt{face\_pressures}, size $d{+}1$), torsion accumulators (\texttt{torsion\_sum}, \texttt{torsion\_sum\_sq} for $\kappa_S$), and a \texttt{simplex\_update\_count}. The implementation does not cache per-simplex shape-quality metrics such as inradius/circumradius ratios. ANN indices are maintained at the node level and refreshed after deferred moves or topology edits that affect local routing.

\paragraph{Rank-1 Oja vs.\ scalar variance.}
The core convergence theory holds with a scalar variance tracker, but the recommended implementation augments it with a rank-1 Oja update of the dominant local-residual direction $\mathbf u_i$. This adds one stored vector per node and gives a more accurate placement direction for proposed splits. For ambient dimensions $d\lesssim 10{,}000$ the memory cost is modest and the split-quality gain justifies it; for very high-dimensional data, fall back to the scalar-only variance with random or Oja-projection-based split direction.

\paragraph{Numerical-stability constants.}
Recommended clipping defaults:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item Set $\varepsilon$ in $\rho_i=\|\mathbf m_i\|/(\sigma_i+\varepsilon)$ to between $10^{-8}$ and $10^{-6}$ depending on data scale.
  \item Bound the per-update centering contribution $\eta_{\mathrm{cent}}\rho_i\|\mathbf m_i\|$ so that a single sample cannot push the deferred accumulator by more than a small fraction of $\delta_{\min}$; the realized move is additionally gated by the $\delta_{\min}$ threshold and the $\|\mathbf a_i\|$ bound below. (The GNG-equivalent rate $\eta_{\mathrm{GNG}}$ of S2.3 is an analysis quantity, not an applied update, and needs no separate runtime clip.)
  \item Constrain spline-flow slopes/bins to avoid near-singular Jacobians during warp fits.
  \item Bound $\|\mathbf a_i\|$ growth between deferred-nudge applications, so a single sample cannot push the accumulator above several $\delta_{\min}$.
\end{itemize}
HNSW defaults: graph degree $M\in[16,32]$, $\mathrm{efConstruction}\in[100,400]$, $\mathrm{efSearch}\in[50,200]$. Increase $\mathrm{efSearch}$ modestly near convergence to improve neighbor stability. Persist indices per recursion level and rebuild only after significant mesh mutation.

\subsection*{S4.6 Numerical Cost Envelopes}
Stage~1 cost is dominated by ANN queries and rank-weighted local updates. Greedy Chaining is local: each accepted simplex pays clique verification and a canonical deduplication insert, with traversal amortized over shared stars. Evidence scoring is local in the affected region and scales with the number of changed stars plus a neighbor ring. Torsion audits and edge-ratio split checks use cached simplex-local matrices and pairwise edge norms. Dual-flow reconstruction runs on the face/dual graph of the accepted complex. These envelopes should be logged by owned object (nodes, edges, simplices, faces, proposals) so scaling bottlenecks can be diagnosed without mixing stages.

\section*{S5. Torsion, Safe Edits, and Warps}

\subsection*{S5.1 Discrete Torsion Diagnostic}
For a $d$-simplex $S=\{v_0,\dots,v_d\}$, let $\bar{\mathbf w}$ and $\bar{\mathbf m}$ be the barycenters of vertex positions and residual means. Form centered matrices
\[
E=[\mathbf w_{v_1}-\bar{\mathbf w}\ \cdots\ \mathbf w_{v_d}-\bar{\mathbf w}],\qquad
M=[\mathbf m_{v_1}-\bar{\mathbf m}\ \cdots\ \mathbf m_{v_d}-\bar{\mathbf m}].
\]
The simplex torsion form is
\[
\Omega_S=M^\top E-E^\top M,\qquad \kappa_S=\|\Omega_S\|_F,\qquad R_S=\kappa_S/\tau^\star.
\]
If the residual mean field is locally consistent with the gradient of a potential, the implied Jacobian is approximately symmetric and $\Omega_S$ is small. Persistent high $R_S$ indicates that local PL geometry is not explaining the residual field conservatively.

\subsection*{S5.2 Torsion Ladder}
The first-implementation torsion ladder uses committed dimensionless defaults:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item \emph{Ignore} if $R_S<0.05$ (numerical noise).
  \item \emph{Keep as-is / monitor} if $0.05\le R_S<0.30$ (tolerable curvature).
  \item \emph{Geometric fix} if $0.30\le R_S<0.60$: propose a torsion-aligned split and accept it only if the S3.4 evidence gate agrees.
  \item \emph{Non-linear upgrade} if $R_S\ge0.60$: propose a local warp. If the fitted warp fails to reduce $R_S$ below $0.60$ on the patch, fall back to a torsion-aligned split, again evidence-gated.
\end{itemize}
The thresholds $(0.05,0.30,0.60)$ are operational defaults, not theoretical constants. Required logs include the distribution of $R_S$, fraction of simplices in each ladder band, accepted torsion-split rate, warp proposal rate, and rollback/fallback rate. Retuning is warranted only if more than $25\%$ of accepted torsion splits immediately trigger the edge-ratio fallback or if more than $10\%$ of mini-NSF patches fall back to geometric splits after training.

\subsection*{S5.3 Split Placement and Edge-Ratio Fallback}
A torsion-aligned split inserts the new vertex along the dominant torsion axis, typically using the longest edge projected onto the principal direction of $\Omega_S$. The proposal must pass the local sample/evidence budget before it is scored. After constructing the candidate children, compute the edge-length ratio
\[
r_{LS}(S)=\ell_{\max}(S)/\ell_{\min}(S)
\]
from cached pairwise edge norms. If any proposed child has $r_{LS}>5$, redo the placement as a centroid star split. This is the only shape-like safeguard in the pipeline; it is local to fresh split candidates and does not require a mesh-wide shape audit.

\subsection*{S5.4 No Per-Simplex Shape Audit}
Proteus does not compute inradius/circumradius ratios, Cayley--Menger determinants, or dihedral-angle quality scores as runtime diagnostics. The reason is structural: variance-cap equilibration drives node hit masses toward a common value in regular interiors, and Hebbian edges approximate Voronoi adjacency at the active scale. The resulting stars are approximately regular without an enforced shape floor. Persistent irregularity is expected only at boundaries and dimensionality junctions, where it is informative rather than a generic numerical failure. Boundary cases are handled by the dual-flow boundary taxonomy; junctions are handled by the stuck-junction detector and freeze rule.

\subsection*{S5.5 Non-Linear Warp Strategies}
Warp proposals are triggered by torsion that remains high after ordinary settling or by broad curvature patterns that repeatedly reject geometric splits. At a non-root recursion level, both global warp candidates and mini-NSF patches are trained in the child PCA frame fixed by S1.4, after any accepted marginal Gaussianization in that same frame. Thus the invertible maps operate on the retained intrinsic working coordinates rather than the parent ambient coordinates, lowering warp cost without discarding information admitted by the child projection audit. Let $P_\kappa$ be the fraction of simplices with $R_S\ge0.30$.
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item If $P_\kappa>50\%$, curvature is widespread and a shallow global invertible map may be proposed.
  \item If $P_\kappa\le25\%$, curvature is patchy and compact mini-NSF patches are attached only to the hottest regions.
  \item If $25\%<P_\kappa\le50\%$, use a moderate global map and mini-NSFs on the worst roughly $10\%$ of patches.
\end{itemize}
Patch identification runs Leiden community detection on the facet-adjacency graph of hot simplices ($R_S\ge0.60$), with resolution auto-tuned so no patch exceeds $P_{\max}=64$ simplices. If a connected hot component cannot be split below $P_{\max}$ by increasing Leiden resolution, recursively bisect it along the longest dual-graph geodesic; if bisection still leaves a patch above $2P_{\max}$, refuse the warp and fall back to torsion-aligned geometric refinement for that component. Each mini-NSF uses 2 rational-quadratic spline coupling layers when $n_{\mathrm{patch}}<5000$ routed samples and 3 layers otherwise; hidden width is 64 below 5000 samples and 128 above; use 8 bins by default and 12 only when held-out likelihood underfits while $R_S$ remains high. Train for
\[
N_{\mathrm{steps}}
=
\min\!\left(5000,\max\!\left(500,\frac{5n_{\mathrm{patch}}}{B}\right)\right),
\qquad
B=\min\!\left(512,\max\!\left(64,\frac{n_{\mathrm{patch}}}{20}\right)\right),
\]
with early stopping after 10 validation checks without improvement. Each warp proposal is accepted only if median patch torsion falls below $0.60$ or drops by at least $30\%$, and held-out log likelihood improves by at least one standard error over the unwarped simplex-local density. Otherwise the warp is rolled back and the system returns to the PL complex or proposes a geometric fallback.

\subsection*{S5.6 Warp / Complex Composition}
Let $P_C$ denote the active PCA projection for region $C$ (the identity at levels where no rank reduction is retained), set $\mathbf z=P_C\mathbf x$, and let $T_C$ map projected coordinates into warped coordinates $\mathbf y=T_C(\mathbf z)$ where the cluster is closer to Gaussian or PL-regular. The complex density is evaluated with respect to the active PCA coordinates:
\[
p_Z(\mathbf z)=p_Y(T_C(\mathbf z))\,\left|\det J_{T_C}(\mathbf z)\right|.
\]
Sampling reverses the path in the active model coordinates: draw $\mathbf y$ from the simplex-local or Gaussian summary in warped coordinates, apply $T_C^{-1}$ into the region PCA frame, and lift through the stored PCA composition when original-coordinate samples are requested. The ambient-coordinate object is therefore a measure on the learned support rather than a full-dimensional density unless no rank reduction was retained. Residuals used by the torsion audit are measured in the active coordinate frame; when a warp is attached, gradients and residual corrections are pulled back through the warp so the explicit complex remains the domain of mass, pressure, and simplex adjacency.

Beyond the basic pullback formula, four operational rules keep warped patches compatible with the explicit complex: boundary identity, unambiguous patch ownership, periodic re-fitting after edits, and ordered composition with any global warp.

\paragraph{Identity-on-boundary constraint (continuity at patch edges).}
A patch warp $T_P$ is constrained to be the identity on the patch boundary $\partial P$:
\[
T_P(\mathbf x)=\mathbf x,\qquad
DT_P(\mathbf x)=I,
\qquad \mathbf x\in\partial P.
\]
This is enforced operationally by (i) including $\partial P$ vertices as fixed pre-images in the NSF training loss with weight on the order of $10^3\,\lambda_{\mathrm{data}}$, and (ii) re-evaluating boundary vertices at training-end and projecting any residual drift back to identity (a single linear correction of the leading coupling-layer bias). Without this constraint the density is discontinuous at $\partial P$; with it, $\log|\det DT_P|\to 0$ and the warped complex agrees with the unwarped complex on boundary vertices.

\paragraph{Containing-patch resolution.}
For a query point $\mathbf x$, locate the containing simplex $S^\star$ in $\mathbf y$-space using the per-level ANN; if $S^\star$ belongs to a patch $P$ with attached $T_P$, use $T_P$ for density and sampling, otherwise use the identity. Patch membership is cached at attachment time and refreshed when patches are re-segmented. Overlapping patches are not permitted: a simplex belongs to at most one patch warp at a given level.

\paragraph{Warp re-fit policy under structure edits.}
Edits inside a warped patch are scored by $F_{\mathrm{DM}}$ in $\mathbf y$-space and applied to the warped complex; the warp $T_P$ is retained until either (a) $K_{\mathrm{warp}}=64$ accepted edits have occurred in $P$, at which point $T_P$ is re-trained with the updated complex as fixed structure, or (b) more than $25\%$ of simplices in $P$ have residual torsion ratio above $0.60$, which immediately triggers a re-fit. Patch boundaries are held fixed across re-fits so the identity-on-boundary constraint continues to hold.

\paragraph{Composition with a global warp.}
When the strategy is mixed (S5.5), a global Glow $T_{\mathrm{glob}}$ is composed first and patch warps act on top in the Glow output frame: $\mathbf y_P=T_P\circ T_{\mathrm{glob}}(\mathbf x)$. Density and sampling chain Jacobians,
\[
\log p(\mathbf x)
=\log p_{\mathbf y_P}(T_P\circ T_{\mathrm{glob}}(\mathbf x))
+\log|\det DT_P|
+\log|\det DT_{\mathrm{glob}}|,
\]
and the patch boundary identity constraint is enforced in the Glow output frame (not in the original data frame).

\section*{S6. Dual Flow and Density Evaluation}
\label{sec:si-dual-flow}

\subsection*{S6.1 Online Face-Pressure Tallies}
When a sample $\mathbf x$ is assigned to a winning simplex $S$, compute residuals relative to the simplex barycenter and project them onto outward facet normals. Each facet receives a nonnegative pressure increment proportional to
\[
\Delta\hat p_f\propto \max\{0,(\mathbf x-\bar{\mathbf w}_S)^\top \mathbf n_f\}.
\]
This fractional tallying avoids winner-take-all face votes and produces smoother boundary/flux evidence for the later conservative solve.

\paragraph{Landed proposal-path stub (OPEN\_ISSUES~\#43; A5-EXP-S61).}
\texttt{proteus.stage2.dual\_flow.accumulate\_face\_pressure\_tally} implements
the increment above behind
\texttt{DualFlowConfig.enable\_face\_tallies} (default off; S14.3), returning
\texttt{FaceTallyResult} (increments, cumulative tallies, barycenter, normals)
or \texttt{None} when the flag is off. Outward normals come from
\texttt{simplex\_outward\_normals}; an operational \texttt{tally\_scale}
(default $1.0$) multiplies increments. The helper is \emph{not} wired into
live sample routing yet --- it is a unit-tested sketch for dry-run / diagnostic
paths only. Do not flip mass/density
\texttt{@awaiting("stage2.dual\_flow")} on this stub.

\paragraph{Stage-1 BMU$\to$face-tally wiring sketch (OPEN\_ISSUES~\#43; A5-T48).}
\texttt{route\_stage1\_bmu\_face\_tallies} behind
\texttt{DualFlowConfig.enable\_stage1\_bmu\_wiring} (default off; S14.3)
bridges Stage-1 ANN winners to S6.1 tallies without calling Stage-1
controllers: each sample carries a node BMU id; candidate simplices are
\texttt{node\_to\_simplices[bmu]}; \texttt{locate\_bmu\_simplex} picks the
winner and accumulates tallies there. Returns \texttt{None} when the flag is
off. Does \emph{not} build \texttt{node\_to\_simplices} from a live Complex /
ANN index --- callers supply the map. Still proposal-path; do not flip
awaiting.

\paragraph{Complex$\to$\texttt{node\_to\_simplices} / ANN BMU incidence
(OPEN\_ISSUES~\#43; A5-EXP-ann-inc / A3-T54).}
Behind \texttt{DualFlowConfig.enable\_complex\_ann\_incidence} (default off;
S14.3), \texttt{proteus.stage2.dual\_flow} lands the missing Complex/ANN
bridge for the S6.1 wiring sketch:
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
  \item \texttt{build\_node\_to\_simplices\_from\_complex} --- invert Complex
  incidence (each top-simplex id recorded under every
  \texttt{vertex\_ids} entry; orphan nodes omitted).
  \item \texttt{build\_simplex\_positions\_from\_complex} --- map simplex
  enumeration id $\to$ $(d{+}1,D)$ vertex positions sliced from
  \texttt{complex.vertex\_positions}.
  \item \texttt{query\_stage1\_ann\_bmus} --- Stage-1 BMU node ids per sample
  via duck-typed \texttt{ann.query\_knn}$(\cdot,k{=}1)$ or naive Euclidean
  argmin on \texttt{node\_positions}.
  \item \texttt{route\_stage1\_from\_complex} --- compose incidence $+$ BMU
  query then delegate to \texttt{route\_stage1\_bmu\_face\_tallies} (forced
  on for the call).
\end{itemize}
Helpers return \texttt{None} when the flag is off. Still proposal-path / not
live-routed; do \emph{not} flip mass/density
\texttt{@awaiting("stage2.dual\_flow")}.

\subsection*{S6.2 Conservative Reconstruction}
For each simplex $S$ with facets $f_0,\dots,f_d$, outward unit normals $\mathbf n_{f_i}\in\mathbb R^d$, and facet areas $A_{f_i}$, stack the incident pressures as $\mathbf p_S=(p_{f_0},\dots,p_{f_d})^\top$ and define
\[
A_S\in\mathbb R^{d\times(d+1)},
\qquad
(A_S)_{\cdot,i}=A_{f_i}\mathbf n_{f_i}.
\]
Thus
\[
A_S\mathbf p_S
=
\sum_i A_{f_i}p_{f_i}\mathbf n_{f_i}
\]
is the discrete divergence-theorem sum over facets of $S$. At steady state on a conservative field this sum vanishes; true exterior no-flux boundary contributions are zeroed according to S6.3.

After topology selection, solve for face pressures $\{p_f\}$ by minimizing
\[
\lambda\sum_f(p_f-\hat p_f)^2
+\sum_S\mu_S\|A_S\mathbf p_S\|_2^2.
\]
Use normalized data weight $\lambda_f=1$ per observed face pressure after whitening $\hat p_f$ by its running empirical standard deviation. The conservation weight is applied uniformly across simplices:
\[
\mu_S
=
\frac{0.1\,\bar\lambda_S}{\|A_S\|_F^2+\epsilon_A},
\qquad
\bar\lambda_S=\frac{1}{d+1}\sum_{f\prec S}\lambda_f,
\qquad
\epsilon_A=10^{-8}.
\label{eq:si-dual-flow-weight}
\]
The first term anchors the solution to empirical tallies; the second penalizes local conservation violations. A count-aware variant may set $\lambda_f=1+n_f/(1+\bar n_f)$, but the baseline remains $\lambda_f=1$ for scale-stable early runs. The quadratic problem is solved by loopy Gaussian belief propagation on the face/factor graph, with damping when spectra are poorly conditioned. Log
\[
r_{\mathrm{data}}
=
\frac{\sum_f(p_f-\hat p_f)^2}{\sum_f\hat p_f^2+\epsilon},
\qquad
r_{\mathrm{cons}}
=
\frac{
\sum_S\|A_S\mathbf p_S\|_2^2/(\|A_S\|_F^2+\epsilon_A)
}{
\sum_f p_f^2+\epsilon
}.
\label{eq:si-dual-flow-residuals}
\]
If $r_{\mathrm{cons}}\gg r_{\mathrm{data}}$ after convergence, increase the constant $0.1$ up to $1.0$; if $r_{\mathrm{data}}$ rises while $r_{\mathrm{cons}}$ is small, decrease it toward $0.01$.

After the dual-flow solve and simplex-mass normalization, log
\[
\epsilon_{\mathrm{mass}}
=
\left|\sum_S m_S-1\right|,
\qquad
\epsilon_{\mathrm{flux}}
=
\frac{\sum_S\|A_S\mathbf p_S\|_2^2}{\sum_f p_f^2+\epsilon}.
\]
Require $\epsilon_{\mathrm{mass}}\le10^{-6}$ after normalization and warn if $\epsilon_{\mathrm{flux}}>10^{-3}$. Persistent flux warnings trigger boundary reclassification or local remediation in the next gate window.
The two conservation residuals serve different purposes: $r_{\mathrm{cons}}$ is the normalized residual monitored during loopy Gaussian BP, while $\epsilon_{\mathrm{flux}}$ is the global post-solve health check on the reconstructed field.

\paragraph{Landed proposal-path stubs (OPEN\_ISSUES~\#43; A5-T44 / A5-EXP-mu/flux /
A5-T46..T48).}

shape the S6.2 API without implementing loopy Gaussian BP on the face/factor
graph (do \emph{not} flip mass/density/benchmark
\texttt{@awaiting("stage2.dual\_flow")}):
\begin{itemize}[leftmargin=1.5em]
